## World Models & Generative Video

- 2026-11-19, **Posterior Matching Distillation**, Hidir Yesiltepe et.al., Paper: [@2611.26629](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26629)
> **Abstract:** Diffusion distillation accelerates image and video generation by compressing a pretrained teacher's iterative sampling process into a few network evaluations. Distribution-based methods achieve this by matching the student's output distribution to the teacher's. This matching is often driven by reverse-KL objectives, whose mode-seeking bias favors dominant teacher modes at the expense of diversity across samples. We introduce Posterior Matching Distillation (PMD), a new member of the distribution-based distillation family that combines mode-covering posterior sampling with any-step generation. At zero population loss, PMD recovers the teacher's full posterior of clean images at each trained noise level. Its stochastic student supports any-step sampling budgets within the trained timestep grid without further training. To learn these posteriors, PMD matches exponentially reweighted averages of student samples conditioned on a fixed noisy state to corresponding teacher posterior means. Under Gaussian corruption, exponentially tilted posterior mean is a single teacher evaluation at a shifted input. We prove that, matching tilted means over an open neighborhood uniquely identifies the full posterior. PMD thus supervises the entire conditional distribution through a family of mean constraints, without any auxiliary student-score network. Experiments on SDXL and FLUX.2 [klein] 4B demonstrate that PMD achieves the best FID among 4-step methods on both models, while maintaining competitive or leading performance across prompt-alignment and perceptual-quality metrics.
- 2026-11-19, **One Prior, Two Dynamics: Repurposing Video Diffusion Transformers for Self-Supervised Latent Action Learning**, Yuxin Jiang et.al., Paper: [@2611.26617](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26617)
> **Abstract:** Videos reveal how the world changes, but rarely the actions that cause those changes. Latent action learning recovers such supervision from visual transitions, yet often learns the underlying dynamics largely from scratch. We ask whether a pretrained video generator can instead provide the prior for both inverse and forward dynamics. We introduce O$^{2}$LaF, built on a simple principle: one prior, two dynamics. We initialize both models from the same pretrained video DiT and adapt them to their distinct roles: a feed-forward transition encoder with learnable action queries for inverse dynamics, and a causal, action-conditioned video generator for forward dynamics. By controlling information access and optimizing both dynamics end-to-end through a compact bottleneck, O$^{2}$LaF learns transferable actions from unlabeled videos. Across navigation and robotic manipulation, O$^{2}$LaF sets a new state of the art in few-shot action probing and enables stronger zero-shot action transfer across visual contexts and embodiments. Beyond generative video priors, we further extend our framework to predictive representation priors such as V-JEPA2, achieving competitive performance. Our findings suggest a broader paradigm for latent-action learning: repurposing pretrained video models to discover transferable actions and predict their consequences.
- 2026-11-19, **Coda-DaVinci: Reimagining Video Generation with Code2Video**, Yiyan Ji et.al., Paper: [@2611.26595](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26595)
> **Abstract:** Code2Video uses agent-authored 3D proxies to guide video generation, enabling explicit control over structure and dynamics. However, how to construct proxies and prompts for optimal downstream quality remains underexplored, as do the capabilities and limitations of this paradigm. Through controlled experiments and blinded human evaluation, we study proxy granularity, physics simulation, and prompting. The results reveal task-dependent trade-offs: coarse proxies favor natural motion, while medium-grained proxies favor consistency. Physics simulation and proxy-aligned prompting further improve average quality. Guided by these findings, we introduce Coda-DaVinci, a task-adaptive agentic framework for Code2Video generation that selects between proxy-guided and direct generation, adapts modeling granularity to each entity, and coordinates simulation with proxy-grounded prompting. Its multi-agent harness preserves scene assets and guides repairs through visual inspection and independent review. Experiments demonstrate leading instruction following on VTR and scene consistency on R2M, improved physical plausibility over direct generation, and higher visual realism than the compared Code2Video baselines. Long-video evaluations further show gains in event structure and consistency across successive interactions, while revealing limitations in transition stability and adherence to scene settings.
- 2026-11-19, **AURA: AUdio-dRiven streaming Avatar**, Nathaniel Cohen et.al., Paper: [@2611.26540](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26540)
> **Abstract:** Current audio-driven video diffusion models can synthesize realistic talking-head videos but generally require dozens of diffusion denoising steps over multi-second video chunks, making them unsuitable for streaming applications such as voice assistants and virtual avatars, where low-latency and online causal generation are required properties. AURA generates video chunk by chunk with a rolling KV cache, requiring only the audio of the current chunk to start generating, rather than a multi-second audio look-ahead like bidirectional models. It comes in two sizes: AURA 1.3B streams 576p video in real time on a single GPU (20.7 fps for a 16 Hz playback rate), making it suited to interactive avatar applications, and AURA 5B streams 720p video at 9.7 fps. Our two-stage distillation pipeline first aligns the causal student with the teacher, by training it to match the teacher's trajectories with fewer diffusion steps and a chunk-wise sliding window causal attention mask, then applies Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD) with backward simulation to close the remaining quality gap. On standard talking-head benchmarks, AURA 1.3B runs 23x faster than its own bidirectional teacher and reaches a quality comparable to TalkVerse at a lower resolution, and AURA 5B matches its TalkVerse teacher at 720p while running 17x faster. To support reproducibility and further research on streaming avatar generation, we release the weights of both models along with a dataset of 30K synthetic videos and their precomputed teacher trajectories.
- 2026-11-19, **Plan4D: Generative Plannable 4D Worlds**, Hangwei Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.26516](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26516)
> **Abstract:** Most world models for future prediction prioritize aestetic quality of generated videos, but offer only weak physical grounding and limited geometric consistency over time, due to the lack of explicit 4D modeling. In this paper, we introduce plan4d, a possible next milestone towards building a generative plannable 4D world model that predicts future RGB videos together with dense 4D geometry, including depth, cameras, point clouds, and scene flow from an observed scene context and text-specified actions. Project Page: https://fogradio.github.io/plan4d-project
- 2026-11-19, **FEA solver integration of a composite World Model with a Spiking Neural Network memory Block for computationally efficient modelling of defects and variabilities**, Bassam El Said et.al., Paper: [@2611.26419](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26419)
> **Abstract:** Each fibre-reinforced composite structure has a unique material architecture. The manufacturing methods used and the presence of defects lead to local variations which require material-scale models that can resolve the response in real time at every location in the structure. Concurrent multi-scale schemes provide this capability, but at a prohibitive computational cost for large structures as the number of integration points and time increments scales with size. Surrogates have been studied extensively as replacements for material-scale models to mitigate these computational limitations. A surrogate must represent an arbitrary local architecture including defects, track the irreversible material state over an unknown number of increments, remain stable when driven recursively by its own predictions, and fit within the memory available at every integration point. Here, a Composites World Model based on a Multi-Modal Spiking Transformer architecture is designed to meet these requirements. The proposed architecture combines a material encoder, a Spiking Neural Network memory block that tracks the long-term internal state of the material, and transformer blocks that learn the short-term applied strain state. The system is trained in three stages: material architecture encoding, base training on short time windows, and recursive fine-tuning with scheduled sampling to suppress cumulative error drift. The trained model is disassembled at inference and coupled to a non-linear FE solver, enabling structural scale simulation of composites containing embedded defects. Benchmarks against experimental results for IM7/8552 laminates under tension, compression and transverse shear, for pristine and wrinkled composites, show that the SNN memory block successfully tracks the material internal state and results in prediction errors between 0.4% and 4.8%.
- 2026-11-19, **ViDecide: Teaching Video Diffusion Models to Reason and Plan Step by Step**, Kaiwen Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.26389](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26389)
> **Abstract:** Current leading video generation models can synthesize highly realistic videos, while still frequently violating physical and logical constraints in multi-step reasoning and planning, even after training with large-scale reasoning data or existing diffusion RL methods. We attribute these failures to the conventional video training paradigm, which treats a solution as a fixed-length clip rather than a chain of decisions. We present ViDecide, a decision-centric framework that trains video diffusion models to predict state transitions causally and decide when to stop, with a decision-focused training schedule. We further propose Corrective Fine-tuning (CFT), which corrects failed decisions by mapping their noise to the model's own successful continuations, achieving stable improvement without policy-gradient optimization. Our framework therefore unlocks greater potential for video generation models as reliable reasoners and planners in the visual world. Project page: https://kevin-thu.github.io/ViDecide.
- 2026-11-19, **OffBeat: Multicoset Sampling for 4D Capture Beyond the Camera Frame Rate**, Chi-Wei Lee et.al., Paper: [@2611.26347](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26347)
> **Abstract:** For 4D reconstruction, a synchronized multi-camera rig records all views at the same timestamps and misses the motion between them. Staggered triggers spread the same frames over more timestamps. However, when the cameras along each row fire one after another, as in 4DSloMo's schedule on the N3DV rig, the row acts as one moving camera that confounds depth with motion. We introduce OffBeat, which models a staggered rig as a spatially distributed multicoset sampler: shifted copies of one sampling grid. From the calibration alone, OffBeat derives camera groups that fire together and their firing order. Since one group records each timestamp, a short-lived primitive can fit an error its cameras share, such as a color offset, and flicker in novel views. We show that a primitive's opacity over time weights all groups equally, for every number of groups, only if it lasts at least one camera frame interval. On Spacetime Gaussians, a minimum lifetime approximates this bound, and a closed-form smoothed box of that length satisfies it exactly, with less flicker than a Gaussian in its place at nearly equal PSNR. In simulated staggered capture on N3DV, OffBeat outperforms the full 4DSloMo pipeline with video diffusion on every test frame, by 3.04 dB in mean dynamic-region PSNR. Between the first and last synchronized frame, it outperforms the best synchronized method of each scene, with or without frame interpolation, by 1.98 dB in mean dynamic-region PSNR, while synchronized capture is better at its own frames. On PanopticSports, in this frame range, OffBeat is comparable in PSNR to synchronized capture with interpolation. On every N3DV scene, replacing 4DSloMo's schedule with ours raises the dynamic-region PSNR of Spacetime Gaussians, 4DSloMo without diffusion, and our reconstruction. Multi-camera rigs could follow faster motion at the same frame count by changing only when each camera fires.
- 2026-11-19, **Online Temporal Rotations for 2 Bit KV-Cache Quantization in Video Diffusion Transformers**, Ryan Lucas et.al., Paper: [@2611.26334](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26334)
> **Abstract:** Video diffusion transformers reuse key--value (KV) caches across denoising steps, which quickly becomes a bottleneck for long video generation. We find that quantizing temporally distant cache vectors leads to a large range and high quantization error, but that nearby video tokens in the KV-cache share a persistent pattern per channel. These patterns also vary across videos, motivating rotations that are adapted to each video's unique cache. We propose Temporal Rotation Quantization (TRQ), a method that rotates frames in the cache that are adjacent in time before quantization. For each block of temporally adjacent cache vectors, TRQ fits an orthogonal transform that maps the block's per-channel means to a vector that is close to a constant across channels. To keep the quantization format lightweight, we parameterize this rotation as a tree of $2 \times 2$ planar rotations, each stored with only two bits, followed by a fixed Hadamard transform shared across all blocks. The rotations are fit without gradients or changes to model weights, and we prove that a simple greedy fit is globally optimal for our 'mean-flattening' objective. The transforms are fit online for each new video, and we release fused Triton kernels to make this process efficient at inference. At 2-bit precision and matched compression, TRQ improves VBench subject consistency over the state-of-the-art method QVG from 90.38 to 92.02 on LongCat-Video-13B and from 86.33 to 87.61 on HY-WorldPlay-8B, as well as improving other metrics.
- 2026-11-19, **SpecWM: Turning Verification into Refinement for Streaming World Models**, Jiaming Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.26211](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26211)
> **Abstract:** Recently, video world models have advanced interactive generation, with large models offering higher quality and smaller models enabling faster inference. Existing speculative methods combine their strengths through fast draft generation and selective target regeneration guided by rewards on decoded frames. However, verification covers only the external scorer's evaluation dimensions and adds decoding and scoring costs. These findings motivate transition-aware refinement. On WBench Navigation, SpecWM improves visual quality at 1.63$\times$ speedup over the target and raises NavScore from the draft's 75.99 to 85.63, achieving the best quality-speed-control trade-off. Additionally, our LongSpecBench shows sustained quality and control over long, multi-turn rollouts, while experiments on Hy-Nomas validate SpecWM across backbones and denoising budgets.

> **Abstract:** Real-time streaming video generation has gained rapid momentum in live content creation and interactive media. However, extending fixed-scale generative video super-resolution (VSR) to varying target scales poses two challenges: downsampling from a larger fixed scale incurs redundant cost, while re-optimizing the diffusion transformer (DiT) is costly and alters the shared generative prior. - 2026-11-19, **StreamEgo: Streaming Exocentric-to-Egocentric Video Generation**, Tianyi Xu et.al., Paper: [@2611.26165](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26165)
> **Abstract:** Exocentric-to-egocentric (Exo2Ego) video generation transforms third-person recordings into first-person viewpoints, unlocking immersive AR/VR experiences and scalable demonstration synthesis for embodied robotics. > **Abstract:** Personalized video generation faces a significant scalability challenge: as the number of custom subjects increases, existing methods suffer from severe identity leakage and semantic entanglement, resulting in videos with blended attributes and incoherent narratives. > **Abstract:** World models can imagine how a physical scene evolves over the next few seconds, but growers need to know how a crop will develop over weeks to months: will this rice plant have headed a month from now, and how will it look at harvest? - 2026-11-19, **Frequency-Synchronized Boundary Coupling for Training-Free Multi-Prompt Long Video Generation**, Yipan Xu et.al., Paper: [@2611.26098](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26098)
> **Abstract:** Text-to-video generation has made remarkable progress with diffusion models and transformer-based video architectures, yet generating coherent long videos from evolving textual descriptions remains challenging. > **Abstract:** World models aim to predict environment dynamics under actions, enabling the prediction of action consequences without executing them in the real world. > **Abstract:** Latent world models predict the outcomes of action sequences, but comparing candidate plans through recursive rollout remains expensive at test time. > **Abstract:** Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies must react while visual inference is still running. - 2026-11-17, **Inertial Video World Models**, Yuan Gao et.al., Paper: [@2611.22314](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22314)
> **Abstract:** The physical world has inertia: it evolves from what already exists rather than being recreated at every instant. ## Computational Design, Mechanism & Fabrication

- 2026-11-19, **A Tutorial on Complementarity-Based Contact Models for Robotics**, Onur Beker et.al., Paper: [@2611.26600](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26600)
> **Abstract:** Simulating rigid-body dynamics with contact is fundamental to robotics, from motion synthesis and control to mechanism design. Many widely used simulators rely on cone complementarity formulations, whose physical assumptions and numerical solution require substantial background. While excellent surveys, monographs, and course notes exist, they mostly address computational mechanics or computer graphics or document a single simulator. To the best of our knowledge, a self-contained pedagogical treatment for robotics, bringing these formulations and their solvers together with complete derivations and a unified notation, does not yet exist. This tutorial addresses this gap by connecting rigid-body dynamics, conic optimization, and frictional contact. In particular, we explain how complementarity conditions encode non-penetration and frictional dissipation, determining separation, sticking, and sliding without prescribed contact modes. We distinguish equivalent transcriptions from convex relaxations, showing how the latter can introduce artifacts such as lift-off and creep. We examine the existence and uniqueness of motion and contact forces, including the solver's role in selecting among multiple solutions. We then compare first-order and Newton-based methods, explain regularization and proximal strategies, and discuss trade-offs between computational cost, robustness, and physical fidelity. Connections to widely used simulators, optimal control, and differentiable simulation help readers interpret simulation results, choose suitable formulations and solvers, and develop their own contact dynamics routines.
- 2026-11-10, **Optimization-Based Path Planning for Industrial Manipulators: Experimental Validation in Additive Manufacturing**, Shiva Ganesh Murali et.al., Paper: [@2611.13983](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13983)
> **Abstract:** Robotic additive manufacturing requires joint paths that reconcile robot feasibility with process-specific geometry. Full-path and boundary-coupled chunked optimization handle references of different lengths. Iterative Weight Tuning (IWT) determines the cost weights from the gaps between Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) and their targets. In carbon-fiber winding and robotic 3D printing, the planned paths meet the geometric tolerances and all KPI targets, and both paths are executed on a UR10e, without extrusion for printing, with joint-level tracking recorded during execution.
- 2026-11-09, **A Unified Interactive Framework for Real-Time Three-Dimensional Cellular Automata Simulation in XR**, Ashkan Rezaei et.al., Paper: [@2611.13296](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13296)
> **Abstract:** Integrating three-dimensional cellular automata (3D CA) into extended reality (XR) requires coordinated simulation, volumetric data management, rendering, and interaction under variable hardware constraints. Its key principles include hardware-aware execution, asynchronous simulation-rendering, adaptive fidelity, and cross-engine interoperability. An analytical evaluation framework is defined to assess performance, latency, scalability, memory efficiency, simulation correctness, and interaction quality. The proposed framework provides a foundation for developing portable and interactive 3D CA systems across desktop and mobile XR platforms.
- 2026-11-09, **Same Physics, Different Gradients: A State-of-the-Art Report on Differentiable Physics Simulation**, Lei Shu et.al., Paper: [@2611.12162](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12162)
> **Abstract:** Differentiable simulation propagates derivatives through a physics simulator, turning animation, system identification, control, and computational design into gradient-based optimization. Engines differ in the physics they model and, less visibly, in which mathematical object they differentiate. We separate two questions the literature conflates. The referent is which object the returned derivative belongs to: the finite computation that executed, the converged equation it approximates, or the continuous dynamics. The realization describes the organization of the backward pass: automatically generated differentiation, an equation-level adjoint solve, or a custom adjoint. The referent defines the target against which exactness is assessed; the implementation determines how accurately that target is approximated. Cost depends on the realization together with how the states needed by the backward pass are retained or recovered. Crossing the axes places every method and engine we classify in a cell, separates claims the literature merges, and shows that the apparent trade-off between exactness and memory follows from how methods have been built rather than from a constraint. A second, orthogonal taxonomy covers contact, where the map is non-smooth. We then ask whether a correct gradient helps. Four mechanisms by which useful gradients are lost are cataloged and measured on one testbed, where the advantage of the exact derivative over the equation-level adjoint depends on where the fitting target comes from as much as on the solver budget, and we review the evidence that zeroth-order methods can outperform simulator gradients in the regimes where those mechanisms act. The model classes, systems techniques and applications are surveyed through this lens, and we close with a reporting checklist, a released diagnostic that computes it, and a research agenda.

> **Abstract:** Dexterous robotic fingers require coordinated joint motion and adaptability to object contact, while independently actuating multiple joints increases the complexity of the transmission mechanism. > **Abstract:** Recognizing geometric texture patterns on 3D models is critical for applications such as heritage protection, model editing, and 3D printing. ## Sketch-based & Creative HCI Tools

- 2026-11-16, **Shaping AI's Beliefs Before It Generates: Human Sense of Agency in Creative AI**, Zi Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.21405](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21405)
> **Abstract:** AI systems for creative media generation expand human capabilities yet risk diminishing human agency. A core problem is anchoring, where the user's thinking becomes anchored to a polished, generated artifact, shifting the user's role from active authoring to reactive editing. We propose pre-generation belief controls that can empower creators to inspect, revise, and steer the AI's interpretation before any media is generated, so that the initial anchor is set by the human. We formalize four design principles and instantiate them in the Proactive Co-Creator (PaCC), a system for early-stage conceptual ideation and prototyping that proactively surfaces its assumptions and uncertainty through clarifications, belief graphs, and media attributes, inviting users to shape the AI's beliefs before generating the final outcome. Using PaCC as a design probe in a small-scale, single-arm exploratory user study, we observed that participants reported directionally positive sense of agency and process control relative to their familiar prompt-based AI tools, noting that surfaced assumptions helped them think through details they had overlooked, whereas their feelings about ownership and authorship diverged
- 2026-11-16, **PromptPoseFX: Authoring Pose-Driven Visual Effects through Augmented Prompting and Interactive Refinement**, Kexin Zheng et.al., Paper: [@2611.20485](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20485)
> **Abstract:** Human pose-driven visual effects offer a compelling way for enhancing video expressiveness and storytelling. - 2026-11-13, **DesignSpecs: A DESIGN.md Dataset of Visual Design Specifications for AI Agents**, Jialiang Wei et.al., Paper: [@2611.17713](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17713)
> **Abstract:** While AI agents increasingly generate and modify user interfaces, they rarely rely on a visual design specification for a certain project. We outline research opportunities on visual design knowledge and design-aware AI agents. Dataset is available at: https://huggingface.co/datasets/Jl-wei/DesignSpecs

> **Abstract:** As generative AI becomes increasingly involved in video creation, creators face challenges in expanding, translating, and negotiating creative intent across stages. - 2026-11-08, **From Sketch to Design: A Cross-scale Workflow for Procedural Generative Urban Design**, Jin Gao et.al., Paper: [@2611.11561](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11561)
> **Abstract:** Urban designers use sketches and symbols to express spatial intentions, while many generative urban design tools rely on numeric parameters that are difficult to relate to this visual process. ## 3D Understanding: Reconstruction & Generation

- 2026-11-19, **Direct3D-V3: Latent-Free Diffusion Transformers in the Native Voxel Domain**, Shuang Wu et.al., Paper: [@2611.26511](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26511)
> **Abstract:** Native 3D generation has largely been built around latent diffusion, with recent advances concentrating on stronger variational autoencoders (VAE) and increasingly elaborate latent representations, from vecset and triplanes to structured sparse voxels. Despite their architectural differences, these methods all share the same premise: geometry is first compressed into a latent space and only then modeled by the generator. We take a different route and propose Direct3D-V3, a latent-free diffusion transformer (DiT) framework that discards the VAE altogether and operates in the native voxel domain, directly modeling raw SDF voxel grids during both training and sampling. To make this direct voxel formulation geometrically reliable, we introduce an implicit manifold regularization over neighboring voxels to suppress non-watertight surfaces, and convert predicted SDF grids into meshes via Marching Cubes so that rendered normal losses can supervise the model directly from its predicted SDF. To improve alignment between the conditioning image and generated geometry, Direct3D-V3 also predicts the camera parameters of the conditioning image as an auxiliary task, encouraging the DiT to learn the mapping from image observations to 3D shape. This latent-free design removes the reconstruction bottleneck, brings training under an objective in the native voxel domain, and yields meshes with substantially richer local detail. The predicted camera and native SDF voxel output further make Direct3D-V3 extensible to generative reconstruction, where diffusion is interleaved with normal-guided refinement to improve alignment with the observed view while the generative prior completes unseen regions. - 2026-11-19, **SGAR: Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction via Sparse Geometry-Guided Attention**, Aoxiang Fan et.al., Paper: [@2611.26493](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26493)
> **Abstract:** Feed-forward models have been very successful in 3D reconstruction with ad hoc attention designs that relate all view patches densely. However, the cost of this dense global attention grows quadratically with the number of views, preventing the model from applying to larger-scale reconstruction settings. In this paper, we argue that most dense attention computations are unnecessary from a classical structure-from-motion perspective: 3D structures stem from geometrically matching regions in 2D. We bring this principle to learning-based feed-forward reconstruction with our Sparse Geometric Attention Reconstruction (SGAR) method. In SGAR, each patch token attends to only a small set of patch tokens from other views, chosen by a lightweight learned key selection module trained to predict cross-view 3D proximity of tokens. Thus, dense global attention is replaced by much more efficient geometric-similarity-guided sparse attention. SGAR is a general method that can be applied to different feed-forward models by fine-tuning. In our experiments, we show that SGAR is the fastest among both state-of-the-art dense and sparse methods and the most accurate sparse one. It runs up to 12-times faster than dense attention at 1,000 views with stable pose accuracy where existing sparse methods fail, and shows the best performance in reconstructing more complete geometries on the same time budget.
- 2026-11-19, **FirstCross: Convex Single-Photon 3D Reconstruction from Sparse Views**, Chi-Wei Lee et.al., Paper: [@2611.26321](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26321)
> **Abstract:** Single-photon lidar records the arrival time of each returning photon, enabling 3D reconstruction from few views and scarce photons. However, leading neural methods such as Transientangelo require over an hour of non-convex per-scene optimization, and their accuracy degrades as photon flux decreases. We propose FirstCross, a network-free approach that instead solves a short sequence of convex problems on a voxel grid. For a solid object, the Poisson likelihood of a ray with given signal and background rates depends on the scene solely through its first crossing, the point where the ray first enters the object. Decomposing this likelihood yields two tractable terms. The first penalizes surfaces in front of detected photons and admits a tight convex closure, namely volume rendering with a running-maximum transmittance in place of NeRF's product transmittance. The second accounts for photons that no surface explains and admits a convex upper bound once the object is assumed to be thick behind each detected surface point. Compared with Transientangelo under its own real-data protocol, FirstCross achieves lower depth error across every test scene, view count, and flux level. With three views and 100 times fewer photons than at the protocol's lowest flux, our mean object depth error stays under half of Transientangelo's with all photons. Given our detected surface points, the best classical fusion has 1.3 to 1.8 times our three-view error across flux levels. On real room-scale captures with 1% of their photons, TransientNeRF has 2.8 to 7.7 times our error. FirstCross lets few-view single-photon scanners cut exposure to 1% and reconstruct an object in about a minute on one GPU.

> **Abstract:** Single-particle cryo-electron microscopy (cryo-EM) determines macromolecular structures from noisy particle images. - 2026-11-19, **PaveSplat: Resolution-Robust Feed-Forward Gaussian Splatting with Residually Corrected Surface-Aligned Covariances**, Janghun Kim et.al., Paper: [@2611.26045](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26045)
> **Abstract:** We present PaveSplat, a feed-forward Gaussian splatting method that adds a learned residual to the analytic surface-aligned covariance, keeping surfels paving the visible surface across rendering resolutions. The surfels then cover the surface loosely; at the training resolution each surfel's one-pixel minimum footprint covers the gaps, but they show up as holes when the same prediction is re-rendered at a higher resolution. On zero-shot DL3DV, it leads PSNR within this group at every scale and ranks first or second on SSIM/LPIPS. - 2026-11-19, **AGSR: Aperture-Guided Super-Resolution for Gaussian Splatting**, Ayman Alkhateeb et.al., Paper: [@2611.25934](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25934)
> **Abstract:** 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables real-time novel view synthesis, but Gaussian rasterization attenuates fine-scale scene structure through the projected covariance of each primitive. - 2026-11-19, **World-delta: Continually Integrating Observations for Incremental 3D Scene Generation**, Haonan Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.25802](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25802)
> **Abstract:** Existing 3D generation methods synthesize visually compelling scenes from fixed inputs, yet struggle to continually expand and revise a 3D world as new observations arrive. Iterative approaches often fuse real observations and generated content into a single representation, treating generated content as established structure rather than revisable predictions. Together, these components enable continual world expansion and revision while reducing redundant generation and preserving scene consistency. Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of World-delta in incremental 3D scene generation, observation-driven correction, scene consistency, and update efficiency over successive scene updates.

> **Abstract:** We propose MergeableRF, a method for radiance field relighting through model parameter merging. We start these networks from the same initialization and introduce merge-aware training, which teaches their parameter combinations to reproduce the corresponding combinations of images. > **Abstract:** Humans can understand the 3D structure of scenes even from highly unfamiliar or extreme viewpoints. Existing physics-augmented pipelines simulate rigid bodies only and cannot accommodate a generated deformable object, which text-to-3D generation returns as a geometrically invalid mesh, sometimes self-intersecting, that has no rest shape, no material model and no admissible initial state. These are the inputs a deformable simulation requires, and we construct each from information already in the pipeline. The rest shape comes from how the object is manufactured rather than from the generated physically inconsistent deformed configuration, and a material library assigns each deformable object its initial elastic parameters, which can then be optimized. The occluded support is regenerated and converted into a watertight collision mesh, each object is released from an optimizable placement above the supporting part that the prompt specifies, and the complete scene is driven to static equilibrium under one unified contact model, with completion verified by geometric checks and a vision-language model. When a scene fails the prompt, a grid search over placements corrects it, and the adjoint of a differentiable simulation of the same contact energy reaches the targets the grid does not, places several bodies jointly and recovers and optimizes material parameters. Multi-body scenes equilibrate in a single simulation, and the pipeline also runs from the text prompt alone. On all sixty-five prompts, SoftPAT3D produces no undesired interpenetration of any kind, whereas every other system does, and its scenes directly support scene editing and robotic manipulation.
- 2026-11-07, **PLS-GS: Probabilistic Lidar Scaffolds for Compact, Surface-Aligned Gaussian Splatting**, Krishnan Harikumar et.al., Paper: [@2611.10699](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10699)
> **Abstract:** While 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is typically optimized from images only, many robotics and mapping applications provide both camera and high-definition LiDAR data, combining rich visual appearance with accurate geometric structure. We introduce PLS-GS, a probabilistic LiDAR scaffold for Gaussian Splatting that exploits this complementary sensing setting to construct a compact 3DGS representation aligned to the measured surface. PLS-GS fits a Hierarchical Gaussian Mixture Model jointly over position and color, yielding a continuous and adaptive scaffold that eliminates the need for discrete scene partitioning such as voxel grids or octrees. The fitted mixture then guides the renderable 3DGS Gaussians: each rendering Gaussian is associated with a GMM component and constrained in the local surface frame induced by that component's covariance. A Mahalanobis support loss bounds the deviation of the rendering Gaussians from the corresponding LiDAR-derived surface. Across five large outdoor scenes, PLS-GS places primitives markedly closer to the LiDAR measurements than both image- and LiDAR-initialized baselines, while employing substantially fewer primitives, yielding a considerably more compact model and faster rendering. At matched capacity, PLS-GS places primitives closer to the LiDAR measurements, with comparable rendering quality. Code is available at https://github.com/k-harikumar/PLS-GS.
- 2026-11-07, **SHIN: Scalable Hierarchy for INcremental 3D Gaussian Splatting**, Reagan Koo et.al., Paper: [@2611.10652](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10652)
> **Abstract:** 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) provides high-quality real-time novel-view synthesis, but deployment budgets vary across devices and network conditions. Most compact methods target one operating point, so another budget requires a separate representation. SHIN is a scalable 3DGS hierarchy with a reusable Base and additive enhancement layers, so each higher-quality operating point extends rather than replaces the decoded representation. Constructing this hierarchy requires deciding which Gaussians each stage adds and how much spherical-harmonic (SH) appearance each retains, because these choices remain fixed in later prefixes. SHIN derives one shared Gaussian order from multi-scale position-scale sensitivity and divides each stage budget between adding Gaussians and retaining SH bands using calibrated gradient-based scores per byte. Lightweight opacity updates adjust compositing while transmitted geometry and SH coefficients remain unchanged. Across three benchmarks, SHIN progressively improves one cumulative representation and achieves the best PSNR-size efficiency in every overlapping comparison with the multi-rate and progressive baselines.
- 2026-11-07, **RefineSplat: Reconstruction-Guided Object-Centric Gaussian Splatting for Open-Vocabulary Querying**, Yiren Lu et.al., Paper: [@2611.10379](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10379)
> **Abstract:** Open-vocabulary 3D scene understanding aims to segment and retrieve arbitrary objects in 3D scenes using natural language queries. Most existing methods typically follow a splat-then-segment paradigm, where 3D Gaussian Splatting is first reconstructed and then augmented with labels or language features for subsequent segmentation. However, this pipeline often suffers from view inconsistency, ambiguous object boundaries, and weak object-level correspondence, especially in complex static and dynamic scenes. Building on our prior Segment-then-Splat framework, we present RefineSplat, a reconstruction-guided extension that retains its object-centric paradigm: it first tracks objects in 2D and then reconstructs each object with a dedicated set of 3D Gaussians, preserving explicit object-Gaussian correspondence for unified open-vocabulary segmentation and retrieval. To improve robustness, we further close the loop between 2D tracking and 3D reconstruction. Since off-the-shelf video trackers can produce unreliable masks that propagate errors into object reconstruction, we introduce a reconstruction-guided feedback mechanism that refines object tracking using rendered masks and multi-view consistency. Moreover, instead of assigning each object a single language embedding, we propose a viewpoint-aware embedding assignment strategy, where each object maintains multiple view-clustered embeddings to capture view-dependent semantic cues. We also introduce Blender-OVS, a synthetic benchmark of static and dynamic sequences with exact object meshes and point clouds, enabling direct evaluation of extracted geometry. Experiments on static and dynamic benchmarks show improved rendered-view segmentation and lower object-level Chamfer distances, with competitive scene reconstruction quality.
- 2026-11-06, **TopSDF: Topological Signed Distance Field for High-Quality Triangle Mesh Extraction from 3D Gaussian Splatting**, Khoi Do et.al., Paper: [@2611.18708](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18708)
> **Abstract:** Recent advances in 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) have demonstrated its effectiveness for photorealistic novel view synthesis. However, extracting clean, watertight, explicit surface meshes from 3DGS remains a critical challenge. Previous methods approximate geometry via discrete Marching Tetrahedra, where each grid vertex is assigned a single scalar signed distance value. At complex geometric boundaries and thin structures, this suboptimal parameterization forces severe compromises across shared vertices, causing gradient collisions and geometric frustration. Furthermore, existing optimization objectives neglect element shape conditioning, producing degenerate triangles. We introduce TopSDF, a framework that models the signed distance state as a multivariate Gaussian random field. By learning latent topological covariances across tetrahedral vertices, our formulation expands local degrees of freedom for SDF estimates, allowing the candidate mesh vertices to be placed flexibly along the bipolar edges in the tetrahedra as driven by such topological correlation. Concurrently, we directly integrate variational meshing energies into the joint optimization, coupled with an energy-driven Gaussian densification strategy to improve triangle mesh quality. Our experiments across Mip-NeRF 360, Tanks & Temples, and ScanNet++ show that TopSDF achieves state-of-the-art mesh-based novel view rendering (+0.86 dB PSNR on Mip-NeRF 360) and triangle-mesh quality (from 0.37 to 0.49 aspect ratio).
- 2026-11-06, **OfficeTwin: Realizing 3D Reconstruction of Redundant Scenes through Partial Viewpoint Perception**, Quanyun Wu et.al., Paper: [@2611.10241](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10241)
> **Abstract:** Feed-forward 3D reconstruction turns an uncalibrated monocular stream into a dense point cloud in a single inference pass, orders of magnitude faster than classical structure-from-motion (e.g., COLMAP), and reliable on the weakly textured, repetitive surfaces where keypoint matching fails outright. However, raw point clouds lack the object identity, physical properties, and watertight volume required for robot learning. This gap widens under robot-native capture. A camera mounted on a robot constrained to a few degrees of freedom sees each object only partially, and the repetitive, weakly textured structure of an office makes distinct workstations nearly indistinguishable, leaving redundant and partial observations that downstream reconstruction cannot resolve. Existing image-to-3D generation methods struggle with these conditions, hallucinating geometry under incomplete views and confusing repeated instances. We present OfficeTwin, a robot-native framework that converts these incomplete observations into simulation-ready digital twins. OfficeTwin first imposes metric and structural constraints, then exploits the office's structural redundancy by registering repeated cubicles into a shared canonical frame, where complementary observations recover geometry occluded in any single view without hallucination. On a real office scan, OfficeTwin recovers every cubicle as an individually watertight, metric solid, outperforming feed-forward reconstruction and generative baselines that fail to produce simulation-ready assets.

## Embodied AI & Robot Learning

- 2026-11-19, **Alignment Drives Reuse: Learning Generalizable Robot-Use Agents from Heterogeneous Experience**, Ziyue Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.26376](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26376)
> **Abstract:** Vision-language models (VLMs) demonstrate broad generalization across tasks and domains. However, comparable generalization in robot manipulation remains challenging. Heterogeneity in robot embodiments, task formulations, geometric conventions, and action interfaces fragments interaction experience across benchmarks and hardware. We rethink robot skill learning through alignment: Can heterogeneous interactions across benchmarks and embodiments jointly improve one shared skillset for robot manipulation? We propose UniCraft, a framework for learning robot manipulation skills by aligning heterogeneous settings. Specifically, UniCraft represents observed scene geometry using consistent coordinate conventions and aligns sensing and control interfaces across robots. It organizes tasks from different benchmarks in a canonical format, allowing interaction feedback to jointly refine shared skills and the orchestration policy that guides their selection and composition, without any demonstrations or model weight updates. We interleave tasks from 3 benchmarks and 6 embodiments, collecting 2,700 learning rollouts per agent model. During training, UniCraft improves pooled task success on unseen scenarios and tasks by 9-28 percentage points on LIBERO-PRO, 10.8-13.3 on RoboTwin 2.0, and 3.1-14.4 on RoboCasa365 (Epochs 1-3). After training, with the learned skills frozen, UniCraft demonstrates zero-shot transfer across 4 new robot embodiments and 5 additional agent models on robosuite and RoboDojo.
- 2026-11-19, **LocoMuJoCo: A Comprehensive Imitation Learning Benchmark for Whole-Body Control**, Firas Al-Hafez et.al., Paper: [@2611.26293](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26293)
> **Abstract:** LocoMuJoCo is an open-source benchmark for imitation learning (IL) and reinforcement learning (RL) on whole-body control. The library couples MuJoCo (CPU) with MJX and the MjWarp backend (GPU) under a single API, exposes 9 humanoid, 3 (musculo-)skeletal, and 4 quadruped embodiments, and ships a curated native motion-capture dataset for every humanoid, to which it can also retarget AMASS and LAFAN1. A modular plug-in architecture makes observations, rewards, control modes, terrains, and domain randomization fully interchangeable, while compact JAX implementations of many RL and IL algorithms provide JIT-compiled end-to-end training, and the companion loco-mujoco-real repository provides sim-to-real deployment. We describe the design, the SMPL-to-robot and robot-to-robot retargeting pipeline, and a usage example. LocoMuJoCo is released under MIT at https://github.com/robfiras/loco-mujoco.
- 2026-11-19, **Fixing the Twin, Not the Detector: Sim-to-Real Roadside LiDAR Detection from Digital-Twin Data**, KuanLin Chen et.al., Paper: [@2611.26035](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26035)
> **Abstract:** Roadside LiDAR detectors are expensive to train on real labels, and digital twins promise to replace these labels, but a detector trained on a twin meets a different sensor, different objects and different annotation conventions on the real site. We describe the pipeline that placed first in the LUMPI track of the UrbanTwin Sim2Real LiDAR Challenge (IEEE BigData 2026 Cup, 0.5541 combined score) and fifth on its V2X-Real track (0.8102). Every detector is a CenterPoint model trained only on synthetic data, on a single 8 GB laptop GPU. Most of the gain came from correcting differences between the twin and the real site rather than from the detector. We align the twin's coordinates, point density and box convention with the test sensor, generate the pedestrians and riders that the twins lack, plus street clutter as unlabeled negatives, and rebuild twin objects at real size and real point density. A non-learned resampling to the per-cell point density of unlabeled real frames lifts LUMPI realism from 0.589 to 0.947, against 0.949 between real frames. Label-free post-processing then exploits what a fixed roadside sensor guarantees: static structures recur in every frame, and real objects have characteristic point statistics. Reporting the validation and test gain of each component side by side, we find that corrections of systematic sim-to-real differences transfer to the test set, typically keeping a quarter to three quarters of their validation gain, while choices that fit the few objects of a validation set do not.
- 2026-11-19, **TipDex-VTLA: Learning When to Refine Actions with Touch for Dexterous Manipulation**, Xudi Ge et.al., Paper: [@2611.25683](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25683)
> **Abstract:** Changes in illumination can induce action drift in dexterous manipulation even when the physical state remains unchanged. Although existing vision--tactile--language--action (VTLA) policies incorporate tactile feedback, when and how touch refines actions under changing visual conditions remains challenging. We introduce \textbf{TipDex-VTLA}, a VTLA policy that combines estimated visual action reliability and tactile forecast verification to regulate execution-time refinement. Specifically, Visual Action Sensitivity Estimation (VASE) learns visual reliability from a frozen reference policy's action sensitivity to illumination perturbations. Tactile Anticipation and Correction during Execution (TACE) compares predicted and measured touch at matched times and combines the resulting discrepancy with visual reliability to gate bounded feature corrections for unexecuted actions. This effectively helps to refine actions under challenging unseen visual conditions. We evaluate TipDex-VTLA on four real-world dexterous manipulation tasks and the UniVTAC simulation benchmark. TipDex-VTLA trained on data collected under normal visual conditions, achieves average full-task success rates of 87.5% (resp. 73.8%) under normal (resp. challenging unseen) lighting in real-world tasks, significantly outperforming some state-of-the-art methods. We will release the collected data and source code.
- 2026-11-19, **A$^2$-VLA: Adapting Vision-Language-Action Policies to New Tools through Agentic Affordance Discovery**, Chenyuan Zhou et.al., Paper: [@2611.25656](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25656)
> **Abstract:** Tools with different shapes can have similar functions, but transferring manipulation skills across tools requires understanding how tool geometry relates to the specific task and adjusting the manipulation strategy accordingly. The key challenge is to discover the common affordances that contribute to task completion across different tools and verify whether they support new tools in performing similar work. We present A$^2$-VLA, a tool-adaptation framework that combines an embodied agent with a VLA policy. It treats tool affordances as hypotheses the agent can construct, test, and revise. Given teleoperated demonstrations with a few source tools, the agent reconstructs the scenes in simulation and autonomously determines task-relevant affordance features: functional regions and their key geometric properties. It develops affordance-extraction skills and refines both the features and skills through autonomous experiments in the reconstructed scenes. Validated extraction skills accumulate in a reusable skill library for unseen tools. Finally, A$^2$-VLA uses encoded functional-region features as conditioning inputs to a $π_{0.5}$ backbone during training and inference. Evaluation examines affordance reliability in reconstructed scenes and real-robot task success on unseen tools. Our method achieves 86.1% success in simulated cross-tool trajectory retargeting based on affordances. - 2026-11-18, **WEVO: World Model-Enabled VLA Optimization for Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation**, Qianzhong Chen et.al., Paper: [@2611.25251](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25251)
> **Abstract:** Reinforcement learning (RL) can improve vision-language-action (VLA) policies beyond imitation, but humanoid exploration is unsafe and simulator RL can exploit residual sim-to-real gaps. We present WEVO, a teleoperation-free framework for post-training a whole-body humanoid VLA in an action-conditioned video world model (WM). The VLA, whole-body controller (WBC), and video WM then form a closed-loop RL environment with dense progress rewards. Across three real robot tasks, play data improves every evaluated WM metric and downstream policy success; video WM RL raises mean success from 23.3% to 75.0%, compared with 48.3% for real robot RL. We therefore leverage the complementary strengths of both environments: simulation provides policy initialization and broad coverage, while the video WM offers a deployment-aligned environment for policy improvement. Project website: https://qianzhong-chen.github.io/wevo.github.io/.
- 2026-11-18, **Ferrofluid Shape Control with Deep Reinforcement Learning**, Anthony Czubarow et.al., Paper: [@2611.25232](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25232)
> **Abstract:** Ferrofluids are liquids containing magnetic nanoparticles, enabling shape control through external magnetic fields. This deformability supports their use as soft robotic bodies that can navigate confined spaces and manipulate objects. Their nonlinear continuum dynamics, however, make reliable control challenging. Existing work shows how spatial magnetic fields from electromagnet arrays can establish and maintain desired ferrofluid configurations. A complementary objective is to exploit the fluid's dynamics under limited actuation to reach prescribed shapes that may not be sustainable at equilibrium. Here, we use deep model-based reinforcement learning to control these transient shapes. These results demonstrate that learning directly from physical interaction enables reliable fluid shaping, opening new possibilities for controlling ferrofluid-based soft robots.
- 2026-11-18, **Phoenix: Learning Scene Structure Representations for Generalizable World-Action Models**, Yupeng Zheng et.al., Paper: [@2611.24938](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24938)
> **Abstract:** World-action models jointly predict future observations and actions, yet reconstruction-oriented visual representations do not explicitly emphasize the spatial relationships and object-state changes needed for manipulation. Our code, pretraining data, and models are available on the project page to help the community reproduce our results.
- 2026-11-18, **3DTouch: A Tactile-aware VLA Model with 3D Geometric Relations for Dexterous Manipulation**, Xin Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.23961](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23961)
> **Abstract:** In contact-rich dexterous manipulation, occlusion by the fingers and interacting objects obscures local geometry, preventing visual observations from fully capturing contact locations. - 2026-11-17, **RFW-S: Everyday Deformable Objects with Independent Simulation Verification**, Jiarui Guo et.al., Paper: [@2611.23056](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23056)
> **Abstract:** Large-scale robotic simulation requires diverse deformable objects with executable physical properties and realistic interaction behaviors. - 2026-11-17, **WATERFALL: Workflow for Adaptive Training with Evolutionary Reward Formulation and Automated Learning Loops**, Eleftherios Triantafyllidis et.al., Paper: [@2611.22854](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22854)
> **Abstract:** Reward design remains a central bottleneck in reinforcement learning (RL), particularly in sparse-reward, long-horizon and partially observable settings requiring dependency chaining, memory, and deceptive affordance disambiguation. We formalise this problem setting by introducing WATERFALL, an iterative population-based workflow for automated reward discovery under the strict observability constraints inherent in POMDP environments. - 2026-11-16, **ASTRA: ASV Simulator for Transferable Realistic Autonomy**, Tsimafei Lazouski et.al., Paper: [@2611.21789](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21789)
> **Abstract:** Autonomy development for marine autonomous surface vehicles (ASVs) remains challenging because field testing is costly and difficult to reproduce. > **Abstract:** World-action models (WAMs) couple action generation with predictions of future states. An initial action chunk may be flawed. > **Abstract:** Natural, coherent full-face dynamic expressions are central to making humanoid robots socially approachable, yet existing systems commonly realize only limited lip motion or scripted expressions. Specifically, we seek to leverage retrieval from domain-mismatched, "in-the-wild" demonstration datasets like DROID to guide a VLA's behavior in a new target domain. To achieve this, we introduce RAGu, which overcomes robotics-specific challenges for RAG: (1) It incorporates simple geometric calibration to transform source demonstrations into the target setup and reduce cross-domain differences. (2) It uses a coarse-to-fine retrieval strategy that selects, from a large pool of pre-warped candidates, the trajectory most compatible with the base VLA. (3) It guides the VLA's flow-matching inference procedure rather than seeking to retrain the base model or expand its limited context window. We evaluate RAGu across real-world, simulation, sim-to-real transfer, and cross-embodiment humanoid manipulation. We find it improves real-world VLA success rates from 20% to 80% and outperforms the closest-related baselines in sim. Project website: raguvla.github.io
- 2026-11-07, **Which Taxels, Not How Many: Tactile Sensor Placement for Dexterous Policies**, Taeyun Kim et.al., Paper: [@2611.10647](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10647)
> **Abstract:** Policies for dexterous manipulation depend on design choices made before training: which sensors enter the observation, where they are placed, and how their readings are arranged for the encoder. These choices are rarely examined. For tactile sensing, simulation studies place taxels uniformly over the hand, and this placement has become a default without being tested. We test this default under a single controlled protocol. The protocol holds the teacher, the training procedure, and the candidate taxels fixed and varies only the sensor configuration and the representation. We evaluate it on in-palm rotation and grasping with three robot hands. A small set of taxels selected from contact statistics matches a far larger uniform set. Taxels at rarely contacted sites are equivalent to no tactile input. With all candidate taxels, their layout in the input tensor does not affect performance, even for a convolutional encoder. Uniform placement therefore overestimates the number of taxels a task requires. We distill these results into lessons for designing tactile policies and into a minimal set of design choices that a tactile policy study should report.
- 2026-11-07, **SimDuoReal: Object-Centric Bimanual Policy for Emergent Dexterity**, Qiyang Yan et.al., Paper: [@2611.10410](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10410)
> **Abstract:** Bimanual dexterous manipulation requires coordinated arm and hand control, yet specifying such coordination manually is challenging and often task-specific. In this work, we explore whether complex bimanual dexterity can emerge from a simple object-centric learning objective. We train reinforcement learning policies under a shared object-centric reward, varying their training environments rather than tuning separate rewards for each behavior. We find that the policies discover pre-grasp manipulation, grasping and regrasping, in-hand adjustment and handover to reach object-pose goals without hand-designed motion sequences. We then refine the experts on transition states and use DAgger and reinforcement learning to distill them into a single policy that composes these behaviors for diverse bimanual tasks. On real hardware, the same policy succeeds in 61 of 80 trials (76.3\%) across four tasks with unseen objects and no task-specific fine-tuning. In simulated barcode scanning, our policy achieves 85.0\% success, compared with 60.4\% for two single-arm policies without handover. These results suggest that this objective provides a simple and scalable interface for learning general bimanual dexterity, shifting the burden from manually designing robot coordination to specifying what should happen to the object.
- 2026-11-07, **GUARD: Anomaly-Informed Q-Steering for Generative Robot Policies in Dexterous Manipulation**, Aryaman Gupta et.al., Paper: [@2611.10343](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10343)
> **Abstract:** Generative robot policies based on diffusion and flow matching can model complex, multimodal behaviors, but remain susceptible to covariate shift during closed-loop execution. Existing approaches mitigate such failures through additional demonstrations, policy fine-tuning, or test-time guidance, but often require human intervention, engineered rewards, or other task-specific signals. We introduce GUARD, an anomaly-informed Q-steering framework that leverages the policy's training distribution to construct a dense, failure-anticipatory steering signal. GUARD pairs sparse task rewards with dense anomaly scores learned from policy training data and propagates both through a learned Q-function. Its gradients then steer the denoising process toward actions that improve task return while preventing trajectories from entering poorly supported regions, without directly modifying the pretrained policy. Across four Adroit dexterous manipulation tasks, GUARD achieves average success rates of $96.0\%$ and $80.0\%$ under nominal and perturbed dynamics, outperforming the strongest baseline by $21.0$ and $20.0$ percentage points while maintaining the lowest anomaly score. GUARD further demonstrates direct sim-to-real transfer on in-hand screwdriver rotation and reorientation tasks, achieving $40$ and $25$ percentage-point gains in success rate. These results demonstrate that anomaly signals can serve not only for failure monitoring but also to complement sparse task rewards as effective supervision for robust policy steering.
- 2026-11-06, **BATCH: Behavior Alignment for Cross-Embodiment Learning from Humans**, Zeyi Liu et.al., Paper: [@2611.10148](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10148)
> **Abstract:** Human demonstrations provide a scalable source of diverse manipulation data, but leveraging them for robot learning remains challenging due to differences in embodiment, viewpoint, action spaces, and temporal structure. We introduce BATCH, a cross-embodiment learning framework that explicitly aligns human and robot behaviors. BATCH learns self-supervised temporal representations with a cycle-consistency objective to establish cross-embodiment correspondences without manual annotation. We further introduce two complementary training objectives that leverage these correspondences within an autoregressive videoaction diffusion model: behavior-aligned co-training and human-to-robot style transfer. On real-world manipulation tasks, BATCH improves generalization to unseen task configurations compared with training on unaligned data and prior cross-embodiment baselines, with additional gains from the style-transfer objective. Our results demonstrate that explicit behavior alignment enables more effective transfer from human demonstrations to robot policies.
- 2026-11-06, **Encoding Memory in World Models with Test-Time Model-Based Reinforcement Learning**, Maxime Burchi et.al., Paper: [@2611.09625](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09625)
> **Abstract:** Model-based reinforcement learning (MBRL) approaches have demonstrated strong performance across diverse domains in recent years, learning accurate world models from agent trajectories. However, despite this progress, current approaches still struggle to surpass human performance on tasks that require long-term memory and planning. In this work, we address the problem of long-term memory in MBRL by moving beyond the commonly studied paradigm of architecture design. We introduce Test-Time Model-Based Reinforcement Learning (TT-MBRL), using gradient descent at test time to encode the memory of episode observations into world model parameters. This enables the agent to learn a distinct world model for each episode, improving memory capabilities compared to traditional approaches that solely rely on contextual memory. We first establish a proof of concept on a simplified two-dimensional variant of the Memory Maze environment, which we refer to as Memory Maze 2D, showing consistent improvements across different world model architectures. We then demonstrate the effectiveness of TT-MBRL in the 3D domain, achieving new state-of-the-art results on the Memory Maze benchmark. Finally, we show that TT-MBRL maintains strong performance on non-memory benchmarks such as DeepMind Control and Atari 100k.
- 2026-11-06, **Probably Safe Sim-to-Real Transfer with Lifetime Validity**, Rohan Tangri et.al., Paper: [@2611.09344](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09344)
> **Abstract:** We present Dejavu, a method that makes sim-to-real transfer of reinforcement learning policies in constrained Markov decision processes probably safe over an unbounded sequence of policy updates. An anytime valid test using per-decision importance sampling and a betting confidence sequence certifies each candidate trained in simulation before deployment using only trajectories collected by a safe baseline policy operating in production. Simulation quality does not affect the safety guarantee, only the production sample efficiency of the method. Finally, a summable per-test failure probability yields the lifetime valid property. Empirical results across three sim-to-real benchmarks support the theoretical work.
- 2026-11-06, **SUGARv2: Learning Diverse Visual Humanoid Loco-Manipulation Skills from Synthesized Interaction References**, Tianshu Wu et.al., Paper: [@2611.09266](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09266)
> **Abstract:** Scaling up visual humanoid loco-manipulation skill acquisition requires reducing the human effort needed as tasks and spatial configurations expand, particularly in demonstration collection and task-specific training design. We present SUGARv2, a unified framework for learning diverse visual humanoid loco-manipulation skills through interaction reference synthesis and a shared training pipeline. To obtain diverse references at low cost, we adapt reusable task templates to different spatial configurations, synthesizing coordinated robot and object motions. To reduce task-specific reward design and training effort, we use reference-based imitation as a common learning objective across tasks. We first train privileged teachers to execute these references, then train visual policies through teacher distillation and reinforcement learning with the same reference-based mimic rewards, using diverse object instances and visual domain randomization to support visual generalization and sim-to-real transfer. Across five humanoid loco-manipulation tasks, SUGARv2 achieves a mean success rate of 91.0% in simulation. Deployment on a real humanoid robot further demonstrates successful sim-to-real transfer for whole-body manipulation. These results demonstrate the framework's potential for learning diverse visual humanoid loco-manipulation skills.
- 2026-11-06, **ToolDex: An Ego-Exo Human Demonstration Dataset for Creative Physical Tool Use**, Mohamed Elmoghany et.al., Paper: [@2611.09036](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09036)
> **Abstract:** Creative tool use is a fundamental human capability and an important step toward general-purpose embodied intelligence. It requires agents to reason about object affordances, physical constraints, and manipulation strategies, especially when standard tools are unavailable. However, progress in this area is limited by the lack of real-world datasets that capture human tool-use behavior with sufficient scale, fidelity, and diversity. Existing benchmarks are often simulation-based, focus on simplified interactions, or lack detailed observations and annotations.   We introduce ToolDex, a large-scale egocentric-exocentric (ego-exo) human demonstration dataset for creative physical tool use. ToolDex captures scenarios in which humans select, adapt, and repurpose available objects as tools. Using synchronized egocentric cameras and eight exocentric cameras, the dataset provides high-fidelity multi-view observations and rich annotations for hands, tools, target objects, and their deformable interactions. Beyond data collection, we establish a benchmark suite characterizing three core capabilities: physical tool-use reasoning, sparse-view 3D and 4D reconstruction, and robot policy learning. ToolDex provides a foundation for studying human tool-use behavior and developing embodied agents that can adaptively use tools in complex real-world environments.   Dataset visualizations and benchmarking results are available at https://tooluse-dataset.github.io/.
- 2026-11-06, **RevoSim: Scalable Multimodal Tactile Simulation for Dexterous Manipulation**, Ke Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.08939](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08939)
> **Abstract:** We present RevoSim, a scalable and high-fidelity multimodal tactile simulator for the BrainCo Revo3 hand that jointly models 285 piezoresistive taxels and five visuotactile fingertips. RevoSim efficiently generates synchronized pressure, depth, RGB, and marker-displacement observations; on a single RTX 5090, its depth-conditioned marker-displacement configuration supports up to 4,096 parallel environments. We train in-hand manipulation and grasping policies entirely in simulation, establishing a foundation for future sim-to-real transfer. Project page: https://braincotech.github.io/RevoSim-web/.
- 2026-11-06, **STRIDE: Learning Humanoid Locomotion in Minutes with Acceleration-Based Rewards**, Nico Bohlinger et.al., Paper: [@2611.08857](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08857)
> **Abstract:** Learning locomotion directly on a humanoid robot is a promising direction to avoid the sim-to-real gap. However, locomotion rewards in Reinforcement Learning (RL) typically track the base velocity, which cannot be measured onboard without an external motion capture system or well-calibrated state estimator. This is why we introduce \method{}, which learns humanoid locomotion from minutes of samples with a reward that only requires the onboard Inertial Measurement Unit (IMU). Instead of tracking the base velocity, STRIDE rewards the robot for following a commanded acceleration profile. To learn efficiently from this reward, we use the off-policy RL algorithm XQC and combine a high gain setting for the joint position controllers with left-right symmetry augmentation. In simulation, STRIDE learns to walk on the Unitree G1 and Booster T1 humanoids within 14 minutes of samples. Compared to learning without these two components, it reduces the falls during training by 81%, while without them the robot learns only in over an hour.
